ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-18-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00038_d8e11192	This brings us to Agenda items 7 and 8 on which the debates are being held under one. ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-18-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00038_d8e11222	Thank you, Madam President. Dear Women Federal Minister! Mr. Federal Minister! We have two main issues to deal with here. The first is a small change, which was also due to the Covid measures, namely that oral nose protection should be used in administrative oral negotiations. It is now to be amended to remove this provision and to make reference to the Regulation in force. We believe that this measure is no longer necessary in the course of such negotiations, of course, in principle right, but the reference to the regulation in force is a law technique that we do not think is correct and good, for we know from the past weeks and months that in part the ministers do not even know what the regulations contain. Then how should the standard subject know what's going on? In any case, such a reference is a very unskillable arrangement and we therefore reject this provision. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] There are other points to be dealt with here: This is about time-limits already imposed by the Covid measures, which are being extended once again. We agree that maintenance advances will be extended even without a request for execution, that maturity dates of creditors' claims will be extended against a borrower who is in economic difficulties, in order to suspend the insolvency obligation in case of over-indebtedness. These are all the right measures that we agree with. It also provides for an amendment to the Rules of Procedure, which is also related to the Covid measures, namely to allow written votes and written elections by the end of the year. On the other hand, there is nothing to say if the whole thing is limited. However, we have a concern, and I will also put forward an amendment which is also related to the Covid measures. This is about short-time work, about the short-time work of professional candidates, more precisely of lawyers. For those, this is an important issue as a lawyer needs to spend a certain period of his lawyer’s career as a professional practice in the so-called core practice, that is, directly with the lawyer – and now the question is: Is the short work taken into account or not? This question is completely open. This will be regulated differently in each Chamber of State, and we have nine Chambers of State. It is partly regulated in such a way that they say the lawyers should make it their own. In part, it is said to be recognized. So it is a completely unclear situation for the attorney himself, and you have to think about it, which is an important point for the lawyer. This is about when he is allowed to take legal proceedings, for which he needs a certain period of practice, and when he can take up his profession when he can become an independent lawyer when he is allowed to become a lawyer. This depends on these periods of practice, and it has not been possible to find a uniform federal regime at the Bars. That is why I am convinced that it is necessary for us to take a legal action. I therefore propose the following amendment, together with Mr Mag. Yildrim and colleagues Dr. Margreiter, with the one in § 2 para. 1 of the Code of Law, which concerns the exercise of the lawyer's office and the practical use in training, is supplemented by the following: The practical use of a lawyer can only be calculated in so far as this activity is carried out primarily and without prejudice to another professional activity; in so far as periods of legal leave or prevention due to illness, accident or a ban on employment under the Mother Protection Act and periods of COVID-19 are likely to be short-time work. So this half-rate is added here, so that it is clear that these times are counted. I am aware that the President of the Bar Association does not wish for this legal arrangement. I very much appreciate him, like to talk to him and very often agree with his comments. It is also far from me to intervene in the autonomy of lawyers or the professions themselves, but if it is not possible to make a uniform regulation, I do not think it appropriate to leave professional candidates in such an uncertain situation. You have to imagine that they can't plan now. They do not know whether these times are counted or to what extent – aliquot or only certain weeks – they are counted. They may find out only weeks or months later, then they may have already agreed to an appointment for the law firm's examination or have already planned when to open their firm. They are in complete uncertainty, and I think it would be fair for young candidates to have planning and legal certainty for all the other regulations that we have now made in connection with Covid. I think that we should not leave nine different regulations here, and these also insecure, and hope that you will go this way together with us, so that we do not leave professional candidates in the rain, but also regulate planning and legal certainty in a united manner.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-06-18-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00038_d8e13482	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! I would like to talk about the transport of live animals, because daily live animals are still transported on the road. This means an immeasurable suffering for these animals, a great burden, and especially in summer they are exposed to extreme temperatures and are often not adequately supplied. The required loading density is disregarded, breaks for the supply are not observed. This animal suffering can only be reduced by regular and strict controls. The problem with the checks is, however, that most of the checks on animal transport are carried out at the slaughterhouses or farms prior to departure – in these checks there are less than 1 per cent of the cases – but the Checks on the transport route itself look quite different. Both should help to make sure that you don't just depend on guarantees that you have to pay back sometime, but that there's really cash for the companies, which nobody else would take over. This is exactly the problem we are facing: You think first of the welfare of your own party and not of the welfare of the country, and that is exactly how you act. They turn around, move around, then there's a press conference, there's a little bit of promotion being announced, then a week later the next promotions, the next pots. – We all know these stories, ladies and gentlemen; and I now give an example. Why is there only one billion euros? – You know, there’s already a suspicion that the second billion will be picked up for the autumn. Entrepreneurs have no desire at all to invest if they do not know how to proceed. On the other hand, of course, consumers also have no desire to consume. For some it was too late, for many others it can be too late, and every day we wait longer, we will cost much, much more. So I think now is really the time for you to finally press the start button, so to speak, so that the money finally comes where it is needed, namely in the companies, so that they really have air to breathe; but you have to give these companies a planability at the same time. Exactly on these two screws you have to turn now, if you want the economy to jump. Which will also be very important: We will perhaps have a problem at the end of the year, namely if the farms have to make balance sheets. I don't think you want it, and if you don't want it – and I take it from you that you don't want it – then you finally have to act differently. This provides for similar arrangements for civil and ground-based civil servants, as well as for voluntary civilians and called-in military soldiers. I would like to conclude by stressing this point, and I believe that we are in agreement: No one wants us to ever get back into a situation where we need to extend civilian servants or call in the extraordinary civil service.

The starting situation of the two groups was naturally different. While the one who volunteered could take into account their professional and family situation, the forced-renewed civilians had to take significant action in their life planning. Most of the around 1,500 civilians, whose use has been extended by three months, have already agreed to return to their established workplace or to a new service relationship. You have to spend another three months with a significantly lower income. Of course, the greater was the disappointment than it was then known that their colleagues who volunteered will receive more €1 292 per month. We have received countless addresses and e-mails in which this has been expressed. However, everyone has made a significant contribution to addressing the Covid-19 crisis. With this request, we want to achieve the abolition of this unfair treatment, which our civil servants have deserved fairly. In the meantime, it has been found that such inequality also exists with the military soldiers. There too we should see that for their extraordinary use there is equal treatment, equal pay. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Stögmüller.]] To date, the Federal Government has not yet been willing to act on this issue, and my previous speaker's speech has also shown that the enthusiasm is limited. I should like to say a few words about these two proposals, which are actually very different: The first request concerns the European Voluntary Service. The European Voluntary Service has not yet been implemented in Austria, as Mr Brandweiner has wrongly said, but it has already existed from 2016 to 2018. Between 2016 and 2018, there was the possibility for Austrian civil servants to complete their civil service as part of the European Voluntary Service. This lasted longer than ten months instead of nine months, then he was counted as civil service. Then something happened in 2018, which was actually just a small thing, which was actually just an editorial change, but which had far-reaching consequences for those concerned in Austria: In 2018, the Juncker Commission renamed the European Voluntary Service (EFD) into the European Solidarity Corps, ESK. These two letters, which have changed, have led to Austria's failure to make civil service abroad, because the competent authorities, in the case of the Civil Service Service Agency, interpret the Austrian Civil Service Act in such a way that this change, which is actually not a change in the matter, but only on behalf of them, leads to the fact that civil service cannot be completed abroad. Now, as well as stakeholders such as the Federal Youth Office say, the authorities could simply interpret the law according to the meaning and even now allow those concerned to do their civil service abroad. That is certainly not how it is handled, and that is why we need this request and we need this amendment. By the way, I am surprised that there is a negative attitude from the ÖVP. In fact, there is a unanimous decision by the conference of the Youth Councils, which should also include some EIA councils and councillors, who demands this – many, yes, if there are many, then it is only right. Now you can still stay outdoors very often, but as soon as groups of people are more common in the interior, we have to expect the infection numbers to increase again. This is also a measure which will provide significant relief and, indeed, an increasing amount of coverage for the farms. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Jakob Schwarz.]] A very crucial and important demand, which has also come from the industry itself, is the area of discharge. We reduce the first tariff levels retroactively to 20 percent. Tourism and also the gastronomy of course live on guests and on holidaymakers, whether from home or abroad. For guests from abroad, we can hope for a maximum of this year, we will not be able to count on them; all the more important this year will be domestic tourism. A lot of advertising is already being done for a holiday in Austria, and according to the latest surveys, many Austrians are ready to spend the holiday in their home country. However, in the situation we are currently in, advertising alone does not do anything. In order to make a holiday in Austria a fact, it takes security, safety, but not only in the area of risk of infection, but security to afford a holiday at all. This is what we are talking about in a number of different companies. We currently have about 600,000 people who have or have been affected by unemployment, and we also have 1.3 million workers who have been or have been affected by short-term work. There are a total of almost 2 million people this year. have less money available. But the situation does not relax. The figures are currently declining. We have dozens of reports that companies are currently closing short work and reporting people to the AMS. Not only the unemployed have much less money at their disposal, but also workers affected by short-time work have much less money, even if the net replacement rate is 80 percent. We come from a high economy; there have been very, very many overtimes necessary and done, and this additional money has been very often invested in vacation. It's missing this year, people don't have it at their disposal. Now it turns out that many colleagues have to get their holiday money to cover their current account, because already so much minus has arisen, as the expenditure has not become less. Nor do those who are still in full employment know whether they will not be affected by other measures in the autumn, and consider whether they are now spending a lot of money on a holiday. In order to support local tourism this year, it takes more than just advertising. It takes incentives and it takes support to make it possible to afford the holiday. These can be gastrouchne, which can be hotel vouchers, free rides for cable cars, cheap train tickets for arrival. There is a need for funds to be put in place, as the Federal Government has to make funds available – the companies, the gastronomy companies, the hotel industry and so on do not have these funds. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Light peace.]] This would create demand in tourism and thus also jobs. Creating jobs is the best way to combat unemployment. That's what someone in here said at the beginning of the day. Many of your measures leave tourism and gastronomy alive – the businesses are practically on the drop: on the drop of VAT reduction, on the drop of fixed-cost subsidies and so on – but they do not create real new demand. It is only those who already have a turnover - but they have to have it in tourism - that is not the case at present. What it needs now are guests and holidaymakers. To that end, we must give people the necessary means and the assurance that they can afford the holiday in Austria as well. Thank you.
